# Schema key audit — reset preparation, 2026-09-16

## Boundary and authority

Read AGENTS.md §0–§3, `.claude/skills/data-modeling/SKILL.md`, `.agents/skills/data-oriented-clojure/SKILL.md`, and all four named authorities end to end: [symbols inventory](symbols-everywhere-inventory-2026-09-17.md), [program-facts PRD](../plan/program-facts-are-the-runtime-prd-2026-09-17.md), [evaluation and retired identities](evaluation-write-path-and-retired-identities-2026-09-16.md), [entity versus pulled shape](entity-schema-vs-pulled-shape-2026-09-16.md). Their specific directions are PRD S2 (`program-facts-are-the-runtime-prd-2026-09-17.md:412`), retirement Option B (`evaluation-write-path-and-retired-identities-2026-09-16.md:515`), and pulled-shape §5 (`entity-schema-vs-pulled-shape-2026-09-16.md:232`). Also read the active plan entry `docs/prds/context-generation/plan/README.md:1`.

Source baseline at entry: `5ae8dcdafed230128aa20bc2ccee73a6faf1d8cf`, with concurrent pulled-ref edits in schema resources and readers. Tables describe the resource bytes parsed during this audit; the per-resource hashes below identify those bytes. No production edits, test JVMs, test gates, cluster restart/reset, other-lane operations, or write evaluations. The explicit no-test-JVM assignment overrides the generic lane gate instruction. Existing foreign changes were preserved; no foreign failure was used as a stopping condition.

`bin/seon status` reported default pid 41413 alive. MCP runtime status answered, with existing problems (2 error signatures, 11 errored evaluations, 90 failed tests); those counts are observations, not a gate result. The sole permitted `eval_clj` call used mode `jvm`, explicit `(seon.operator/connection "default")`, and `read_only: true`:

```clojure
(let [conn (seon.operator/connection "default")
      db (seon.db/db conn)
      forms (seon.schema.edn/packaged-forms)
      selected (for [[k f] forms
                     :when (and (vector? f) (= :map (first f))
                                (map? (second f)) (:seon.db/attributes (second f))
                                (some #(and (vector? %)
                                            (= :seon.cluster.eval/id (first %)))
                                      (drop 2 f)))] k)]
  {:evaluation-write-schema-candidates (vec selected)
   :evaluation-identity
   (seon.db/pull db [:db/ident :db/unique]
                 [:db/ident :seon.cluster.eval/id])})
;; Returned in 39 ms:
;; {:evaluation-write-schema-candidates [:seon.cluster.eval/receipt]
;;  :evaluation-identity {:db/ident :seon.cluster.eval/id
;;                        :db/unique :db.unique/identity}}
```

This observes packaged declarations and installed identity, not a malformed-write experiment. The actual validator selection claim additionally follows the opened implementation at `src/seon/db.clj:2683`. No second evaluation was spent.

## Reading the tables

Each table covers **every map entry**, including imported namespaces. `W` names the constructor/mutator chain immediately above that table, with function and source line. An upsert writer may omit an already-present required field; “always” means the complete newly constructed entity, not every partial update. “Sometimes” means a conditional, copied optional input, lifecycle arm, or empty relation. It does not mean that omission is correct. Recommendations to require a key apply at complete construction and the final writer, not to every patch map.

All entities can also enter through the generic admitted transaction route: `seon.db/transact!` → attribute checks and `write-report-error` (`src/seon/db.clj:2992`, `src/seon/db.clj:3213`); initialization rows are forwarded by `seon.config/population-transaction-data` / `apply-compiled!` (`src/seon/config.clj:422`, `src/seon/config.clj:454`) and `seon.cluster/transact-initialization!` (`src/seon/cluster.clj:1239`). Program reconciliation uses `seon.program/replacement-tx` and `seon.fn/reconcile-tx-in` (`src/seon/program.cljc:1007`, `src/seon/fn.clj:2557`). These generic writers can carry **any** declared keys: their existence does not establish that a dedicated constructor supplies a key. Tables name dedicated constructors plus relevant mutators; schema-mediated forwarding is explicitly identified when there is no literal assignment in those functions. Arbitrary caller-built maps cannot be exhaustively enumerated statically; the write invariant must cover them too.

Reader cells name an opened consumer and, where found, its exact key-access line. A plain key read is not itself a proof of absence semantics: the proposed absent condition is stated in the recommendation and must be made the declaration's docstring. Required sparse collection handling is the cross-cutting admission issue above, not a reason to mark every relation optional. `:db/id` and reader-added `:t` are projection data, not newly required stored facts.

The no-required-identity set contains **29** marked maps. Their property contributes storage attributes; it does not by itself cause `write-entity-schemas` to select them (`src/seon/db.clj:2683`). Some are observations later carried by a result entity, not independently identified entities. That is why the list is a validation-coverage finding, not an instruction to stamp every map with a new identity.

### Reproduction (Babashka; no JVM)
```clojure
(require '[clojure.edn :as edn] '[clojure.java.io :as io])
(let [files (sort-by str (filter #(.endsWith (str %) ".edn")
                                 (file-seq (io/file "resources/seon/schemas"))))
      rows (for [file files [key form] (edn/read-string (slurp file))
                 :when (and (vector? form) (= :map (first form))
                            (map? (second form))
                            (true? (:seon.db/attributes (second form))))]
             {:file (str file) :key key :entries (vec (drop 2 form))})]
  (prn {:maps (count rows) :files (count (set (map :file rows)))
        :entries (reduce + (map (comp count :entries) rows))})
  (doseq [row (sort-by :key rows)] (prn row)))
```

This is an EDN parse, not a regex approximation. Line positions were read from the resource forms using Babashka's `edamame.core/parse-string-all` metadata; source searches included both literal identity/namespace keys and alias-qualified usages. The audit's temporary extraction tools were removed after generating this document. No executable probe result depends on an uncommitted script.

| `:seon.wake/unanswered` | `resources/seon/schemas/seon.wake.edn:31` | **No for this declared shape**: request/response, aggregate, or reader projection. Identity-valued members reference the stored entities; marking this map would impose its view/request requirements on writes. |

### Broader coverage boundary

Top-level maps without an identity-bearing member or `:db/id` are not inferred to be stored entities just from a `/value` or `/result` suffix. In particular, `:seon.error/value` is a flat failure envelope (`resources/seon/schemas/seon.error.edn:1`), not the identified `:seon.error/error`; maintenance operation results are projected by `result-entity` and `project-*-result` (`src/seon/maintenance.clj:37`, `src/seon/maintenance.clj:115`, `src/seon/maintenance.clj:141`, `src/seon/maintenance.clj:177`). Their marked persistence component maps are already included above. Config composites are generated from leaf declarations by `seon.schema.edn` (`src/seon/schema/edn.clj:67`), so a top-level resource census is not a census of every runtime-generated schema. This audit's 74/621 numbers describe exactly the resources requested, not a claim that every database entity has a marked schema.

## Cross-key findings that a single row cannot express

| Class | Finding and recommendation | Opened evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Required derivables | Require function `source`, `arglists`, `private?`, `calls` on the defined arm. `var-row` supplies the first two but drops empty calls. Require test namespace, source and computed calls on the test declaration arm. Other relations (`references`, `call-arities`, `writes`, `keywords`) have computed empty cases too: require their complete logical construction when S1 supplies their analysis, with the same empty-collection admission rule. Do not equate “no datoms” with “analyzer ran.” | `src/seon/fn.clj:584`, `src/seon/fn.clj:647`, `src/seon/program.cljc:899`, `src/seon/program.cljc:906`, `src/seon/db.clj:2895` |
| Retirement | Implement the ruled `retired-tx` arm at the existing canonical declaration/write validator. `write-entity-schemas` currently accepts only a map shape; replacing the schema with a bare `:or`/`:multi` without updating schema discovery would remove validation entirely. Teach discovery the declared arms in the same publication. Clear retirement on a genuine redefinition. R3 must not forge namespace/source to masquerade as a defined row. For never-defined referenced names, a positive `referenced-tx` fact is a recommendation requiring design review, not an already-ruled attribute. | `src/seon/db.clj:2683`, `src/seon/db.clj:2913`, `src/seon/program.cljc:1007`, `src/seon/cluster/source.clj:305` |
| Retired test/schema/namespace identities | The retirement writer operates all program identity families; do not fix only fn/sym and leave test/schema deletion on absence-selected validation. Derive each family's definition obligations from its declaration. An exact positive retirement attribute across these families needs a single declared design; this table does not invent several competing names. | `src/seon/program.cljc:1049`, `src/seon/turn.clj:1280`, `src/seon/fn.clj:2557` |
| Evaluation whole-write validation | Current receipt map is selected, but most outcome fields are individually optional. Consolidate it under the canonical evaluation schema and require coherent groups: id/run/ordinal/at at creation; shown text OR explicit error/interruption at terminal settlement; renderer/origin only for generated values; read evidence only for reads. Keep comments capable of having no executable source. Use the same writer validator for native datoms, expanded `:db.fn/call`, retractions and component consequences. | `resources/seon/schemas/seon.cluster.eval.edn:29`, `src/seon/turn.clj:1495`, `src/seon/turn.clj:1569`, `src/seon/turn.clj:1712`, `src/seon/turn.clj:1803`, `src/seon/db.clj:2992` |
| Ref validation | Keep logical transaction refs separate from resolved datom values and pull results. A reader selector determines nested maps/vectors. Remove per-attribute inline `[:map [:db/id :int]]` alternatives once the derived reader forms land; keep the in-flight shared-ref widening until that replacement works. Do not delete legitimate nested-map transaction syntax. | `src/seon/db.clj:2749`, `src/seon/db.clj:2774`, `reference-code/datahike/src/datahike/db/transaction.cljc:739`, `reference-code/datahike/src/datahike/pull_api.cljc:333`, `reference-code/datahike/src/datahike/pull_api.cljc:345` |
| Nil/any/some/maybe | Expanded entity entry forms expose `:any` through `my.plan.item/done-query`, `datahike.read/dependency-plan`, `seon.db/read-request`, and `seon.listen/value`. These are query/literal grammar boundaries, not permission to store nil at the attribute root. Specify query/read-plan/EDN literal grammar and justify arbitrary nested literal positions; reject top-level nil and malformed plans. `:nil` inside the key-kind enum is a *keyword naming a literal key kind*, not a nilable attribute. No direct `[:maybe ...]` entry was found in the 621-entry parsed inventory. | `resources/seon/schemas/my.plan.item.edn:1`, `resources/seon/schemas/seon.listen.edn:3`, `resources/seon/schemas/seon.cluster.eval.edn:208`, `resources/seon/schemas/seon.schema.map-entry.edn:1`, `src/seon/schema/datahike.clj:169` |
| Kind/type stamps | `fn.ast/type` and `schema.shape/type` describe Malli operators; `test.failure/type` is the assertion event's fail/error state; `lint/type` is clj-kondo's diagnostic category; `error/kind` identifies a failure class. Keep the dependency fact with an explicit docstring rather than deleting it solely because its key is named type. `schema.map-entry/key-kind` duplicates classification available from typed values/EDN; derive it or enforce it against those facts. None should be used as an entity-table selector. | `src/seon/program.cljc:524`, `src/seon/fn/schema_shape.clj:149`, `src/seon/fn/schema_shape.clj:257`, `src/seon/test/runner.clj:210`, `src/seon/fn.clj:1171` |
| Existing positive lifecycle facts | Keep turn closed-tx, plan completed-tx, issue resolved-tx/budget-exhausted-tx, message read-tx, maintenance completed-at/interrupted-at and effect settled-at/interrupted-at optional with precise before/after conditions. Their absence is the complement of an explicit transition. Do not add “open?” or “healthy?” booleans. Require result/error coherence when terminal evidence exists. | `src/seon/turn.clj:193`, `src/seon/plan.clj:702`, `src/seon/issue.clj:927`, `src/seon/schedule.clj:419`, `src/seon/schedule.clj:462`, `src/seon/effect.clj:43` |
| Recorded test results | Test definition and latest result share the identity. Requiring result counts on every unrun test is wrong; require pass/fail/error counts, run basis, run time and run ref together when a result exists. Do not let missing counts turn into zero and thereby pass. Keep absent failure collections as empty only after the completed run is established. | `resources/seon/schemas/seon.test.edn:55`, `resources/seon/schemas/seon.test.edn:77`, `src/seon/test/runner.clj:2092`, `src/seon/test/runner.clj:2242` |
| Optional because a writer omits it | SCI binding-rows conditionally omits import target-class whereas indexing supplies it. Make the target required for a usable import and surface unresolved target as a diagnostic. Agent creation supplies namespace/plan/settings/runtime; require them on a complete agent. Plan insertion supplies position; require it. Capture contribution constructor supplies block/name/hash/tokens; require them on that construction. The existing `append-tx` constructor instead supplies agent/evaluations and no capture fields: keep that explicit selection arm, with its agent required (`src/seon/context.clj:160`). | `src/seon/sci/eval.clj:529`, `src/seon/fn.clj:293`, `src/seon/cluster/agent.clj:122`, `src/seon/plan.clj:516`, `src/seon/context.clj:468` |

## Reset edit list grouped by resource

These are the launch units. “Document optional” means update the leaf declaration's docstring to name the absence condition in its per-key row; do not blindly relax or require it. A required key change and every full constructor it affects must publish together. Existing partial updates remain valid if the final entity satisfies the schema. Symbols, required derivables, retirement and pulled-shape changes share the **one reset** authorized by the owner.

### Writer changes shared by those resource edits

| Change | Exact owners / obligations |
|---|---|
| Symbols | Indexer `var-row` and call graph construction (`src/seon/fn.clj:584`); canonical/agent declarations (`src/seon/program.cljc:906`, `src/seon/sci/eval.clj:392`); lookup/ref minting (`src/seon/cluster/source.clj:305`); test recording (`src/seon/test/runner.clj:2092`); effect owner refs (`src/seon/effect.clj:757`); schedule portfolio (`src/seon/schedule.clj:46`); citation resolution (`src/seon/issue.clj:157`); activation requested-symbol set currently `map str` (`src/seon/cluster.clj:1382`). Audit all transitive aliases, especially activation/executable-symbols, not just direct string declarations. Preserve `:seon.search/index :symbol`: it selects identifier tokenization (`src/seon/search.clj:170`). |
| Required rows | Both source indexing and SCI declarations must use the same analysis and constructor. `program/declaration-required-attributes` is a second list already requiring facts the schema calls optional; move those obligations into the canonical schema instead of extending this list (`src/seon/program.cljc:899`). |
| Positive retirement | `seon.program/replacement-tx` (`src/seon/program.cljc:1007`), `seon.turn/row-tx` (`src/seon/turn.clj:1280`), `seon.fn/reconcile-tx-in` (`src/seon/fn.clj:2557`), and evidence minting (`src/seon/cluster/source.clj:305`). Ordinary redefinition must remove retirement; evidence-only minting must not fabricate a historical retirement transaction. |
| Whole-entity coverage | `write-entity-schemas` must retain its obligations through schema-arm changes and component traversal; `write-report-error` must continue validating every changed entity including retraction consequences (`src/seon/db.clj:2683`, `src/seon/db.clj:2992`). A vanished required ref must refuse the transaction, not appear as healthy missing data. |
| Exact supplied refs | Replace `write-value`'s fake `0` with grammar-aware validation (`src/seon/db.clj:2774`). Retain Datahike's authority over tempid/lookup/nested entity resolution (`reference-code/datahike/src/datahike/db/transaction.cljc:739`, `reference-code/datahike/src/datahike/db/transaction.cljc:946`). This is one writer-path change, not a second writer. |
| Pulled forms | Derive from canonical entity schema plus selector using the projection's own registry. Component auto-expansion and cardinality-many vectors are Datahike behavior (`reference-code/datahike/src/datahike/pull_api.cljc:345`). Dynamic-selector readers must use the same selector value for pull and validation. No hand-written second entity schemas. |

## Verification and landing

The audit is source research, not an implementation proof. Inventory coverage is checked by parsing every schema resource and comparing table entry counts to the parsed map entries. No test JVM or gate was run, as instructed. The one live read confirmed a candidate write schema and identity installation, not all writer behavior. Recommendations concerning conditional arms must be proven by the implementation lanes on the canonical armed harness after the one reset; this document claims no such proof.

The audit records its findings here, within the requested research-only deliverable; no production, issue index, or other lane's files were edited. The two inaccuracies in prior research are explicitly corrected above rather than attributed to a foreign code defect. No task was delegated.
